# DEDICATION

Prabhata Samgiita
1400 (21/03/1984)

NIJERA CHANDA HÁRIYE PHELECHI
TOMÁR CHANDE NÁCITE CÁI
BHÁVA BHÁSÁ SUR KICHUI NÁHIKO,
TABU TAVA GUŃA GÁN JE GÁI,
TOMÁR CHANDE NÁCITE CÁI

AVYAKTER CHILO JATA BHÁVA,
VYAKTÁVYAKTE JATA MAHÁBHÁVA
SABÁI TOMÁR CARAŃA O PRÁNTE
TÁI O CARAŃE NATI JÁNÁI,
TOMÁR CHANDE NÁCITE CÁI

KICHUI ÁSI NI NIYE SÁTHE KARE
JÁBO NÁ KICHUI MANJÚSÁ BHARE,
MOR JATA PÁPA MOR THEKE JÁK
PÚŃYER MÁLÁ GALE PARÁI,
TOMÁR CHANDE NÁCITE CÁI
NIJERA CHANDA HÁRIYE PHELECHI
TOMÁR CHANDE NÁCITE CÁI.

***

Completely losing my own personal rhythm,
To Your rhythm and melodies alone
O, I want to dance.
I have no ideas, ideation or any melody at all;
But still, how I long to sing
Of Your sweet, noble qualities
And all the secret characteristics of Your Form.

All unexpressed ecstasy,
All the supreme ecstasy of Your touch
In all its inexpressible expressions.
Lies at the altar of Your feet.
Hence, unto Your Feet, I bow and fall in surrender.

Nothing fancy have I regarded as my own;
And nothing shall I take to fill my jewelbox.
All sins remaining mine,
I shall place the result of my good actions,
As a garland around Your neck.

# COSMIC MIND
Conoscenza in TrasFormazione

Cosmic Mind is a cooperative dedicated to the physico-psycho-spiritual growth of human beings and to the social and cultural growth inspired by neohumanism.

Cosmic Mind offers courses, seminars, books, guidance and spiritual teachings, conferences and events both to individuals and to public and private organisations.

The projects are run by a team of people with different skills, collaborating with a range of academic institutions as well as global associations who are working towards a peaceful, progressive world, through the sharing of philosophies, traditions and innovative ideas between East and West.

Cosmic Mind publishes a selection of books which highlight the innate dignity and dynamic spirit of our human nature.

# PREFACE

> **_TAVA SMRITITE MÁDHURII VARAŚÁY_ (3798)**
> **In memories of You, sweetness rains down.**

These sweet lines from Prabhat Samgiit reveal this book in a nutshell. So much sweetness keeps pouring down upon me from remembering His love. These stories are a few drops that I have collected and offered unto you.

This book was made possible due to the help of many people, especially those who participated in these events. I offer my thanks to every single one of them and I want to thank my Baba for providing me with so many loving brothers and sisters in this life from all over the world. I especially want to thank Shrii Prabhakar, Acarya Kamaleshvara and sister Krsnapriya for kindly taking the time to edit these stories. I also would like to thank Shrii Devashish for his detailed, fine editing. I want to thank you, the reader, for taking the time to read about our Baba who loves you so much.

I want to say clearly that neither can I express the glory of Baba's spiritual refulgence, nor the bliss of His love. But I am bound to express all of this in everything I do and in these stories as well. Stories come all the time in my heart and move me more than I can ever hope to say or write. Baba expressed this in His songs saying,

> ***SHEFÁLII SUVÁSE BHÁSE***
> ***TAVA PRIITI KUSHE KÁSHE***
> ***SHARATER RÚPÁLI MEGHE***
> ***SHARATER RÚPÁLI MEGHE,***
> ***TOMÁRII SMRITI JE JÁGE***
> ***SARÁYE DEY SE ÁNDHÁR,***
> ***VASUDHÁR JATA VYATHÁ BHÁR***
> ***TUMI PRIYA SABÁKÁR (2839)***

> Like night jasmine fragrance,
> Your love floats in tall grasses.
> With silvery autumn clouds,
> Memories of You arise and
> That wipes away the darkness
> And all burdens of agony
> On this earth of ours.

These stories come back to my mind most especially when I see the suffering in the world today and when I realize the terrifying suffering to come in the future. Even the smallest suffering brings to my mind the memories of All-Compassionate Baba. As He says so poignantly,

> ***JHARÁ PÁTÁ KÁNDE VANE,***
> ***VYATHÁ JÁGÁY JE MANE***
> ***HÁRÁNO DINER SMRITI,***
> ***ÁSE JE SAḾGOPANE (920)***

> Dropped leaves cry in the forest
> Awakening such agony in my mind
> As memories of those lost days of ours
> Come back again without anyone knowing.

Who can understand this sweet heartache for Him while living in this world? Whatever we may do, we are always just waiting for Him. Whether I lie down or travel or talk to devotees — it is only just more and more ways of waiting for Him. Read these stories and open your heart to His love and then you will sing like I do,

> ***SMRITI BUKE NIYE BASE ÁCHI***
> ***PHELE ÁSÁ SEI HÁRÁNO DINER***
> ***SEI HÁRÁNO DINER***
> ***AINJAN ENKE COKHE JEGE ÁCHI***
> ***PRATIIKŚÁ TAVA ÁGAMANER***
> ***TAVA ÁGAMANER (1220)***

> I only just keep sitting
> With my heart full of those memories,
> Of those lost days when You came.
> O those lost days.
> With my eyes anointed with these memories
> I remain always awake
> For Your arrival
> For You to come back to me.

# PROLOGUE

I don't know why and how it happened that a boy of my nature, who had a reputation of always being argumentative in nature, put one foot into the ocean of spirituality by learning meditation. I always believed in my heart that the Almighty was everywhere and rested my faith on Thakur (Krsna).

There was a temple of Shiva just fifty feet from the house where I lived and grew up. There was no priest assigned to take care of that temple. I used to swim regularly in the big pond there and I used to peep inside at the Shiva lingam (idol), not to pray but to find out whether some worshippers had left any coconut or fruits as offerings to Shiva. If anything was lying there, I would open the door and with a sweet smile bow to the idol, grab those fruits with both hands and come out with eyes twinkling. When there were a lot of offerings, I used to offer thanks saying, "Bhole Baba" (Shiva who remains oblivious to all our faults) because Shiva was everything to me. If there was very little or no offerings, then I used to scornfully say that He was sometimes a miser, for not bestowing an abundance of offerings at all times. I had great love for Him.

At the other end of my village stood a black statue. The villagers used to address it as Bhairava. I wondered why he was there at the end of the village along the path leading to the cremation ground. I was told that as Bhairava, his duty was to protect the village and the villagers. He was a guard ready to protect us from bad omens. Once a cholera epidemic spread in our village and within three days, eight people died. The villagers went to the statue of Bhairava with the priest and with folded hands prayed to him to save the village.

Once I asked an elderly man in the village about Bhairava. Why was he black in colour and why did he keep standing under the open sky? There was no roof over his head and no clothes on his

body. He is a bhairava, his place is in the cremation ground and he remains awake all night. This is what I was told in reply.

I was not able to find a link between Shiva who was the presiding deity of my village and Bhairava who was naked and who stayed outside the village in order to protect it. No roof to live under, no clothes to wear, no offerings to eat; his job was to protect the village—to protect humanity. This realization made a deep impression in my mind. What a great role this Bhairava had. I was too small to understand all this until Baba made me a bhairava by His boundless grace when I became an avadhuta.

With the passage of time, I grew to be naughty and rough. I hardly slept during the hot summer time as I was moving from place to place. When I had some accidents, like falling from a tree and being injured while crossing the railway track, my mother would always offer thanks saying, "Bhole Baba, You saved him."

I was admitted to a Christian school in another village that was larger and was across the railway line. I was older than the required age for enrollment in first standard. Thus my family gave papers stating that I was four years younger than I really was. The happiest person was my mother who was free from my mischief of playing pranks in the pond and stealing fruits from our neighbours' gardens.

I was always an adventurous boy and hence was able to help people on certain occasions by His grace. Once when I and five other students went to swim in the river Kharun, we jumped in and one boy was not a good swimmer. We saw that he was struggling as he was swept far away by the current. So I jumped in, caught hold of him and brought him to the shore.

Years later, in Dharma Maha Cakra (DMC) in Kanpur in 1979, Professor Surender from Moga, Punjab wanted to cross the river Ganges. Halfway across, while swimming, he started struggling. We all noticed that he seemed to be drowning. I jumped in and swam to him. Then I took his hand and after great struggle took him to the shore.

After completing middle school I was sent to the city and got a room in a hostel. It was here that an unseen destiny, managed and controlled by unseen hands, revealed itself and the Master of my destiny placed His holy Hands upon my head.

It is not true to say that I met Him. It was He who made me meet Him. I never thought of becoming an avadhuta and it was He who made me and shaped me as per His sweet will. I deserve no credit at all and no appreciation.

Salutations unto Baba that I am now here, writing my feelings through these stories offered unto Him.

# BEGINNING

It was Friday morning, 7 October 1961. The proctor of the hostel where I was staying announced that there would be a meeting in the evening and everyone should be present. The meeting was to be addressed by an expert yogi. The stranger wanted to discuss about the science of mental concentration as an essential instrument for short- and long-term memory. As a young boy of around seventeen years, I was also fascinated with this subject. But when I saw the man — a bearded acarya — I immediately changed my opinion and decided not to attend this meeting. The idea at the back of my mind was that this acarya with long hair and beard would ultimately work for his own vested interests. As a science student, I had my own idea and conception of God. God was everywhere, all the time and takes care of everyone, small or big. He is all-knowing and hence nothing is beyond Him. After seeing the stranger, I decided not to attend the meeting. It would be a sheer waste of time. Better to remain confined to my studies, as the examination was nearing. As a meritorious student, I wanted to devote my time in study instead of pointlessly wasting the time.

*My mind would look back to two incidents of early years. Firstly, being the youngest in the family, once I was asked by my mother on Diipavalii night to go around the village's outskirts and crack coconuts — one for each of the shrines of gods and goddesses in different parts of the village. It was, and perhaps still is, the firm belief of people that these various deities can possess people, grant boons, protect the village from calamities, bring prosperity, etc. It was considered to be a sacrilege if anyone did not pay respect with coconut and did not offer flowers, fruits, grains — especially the first crops from the fields. My mother asked me to go and offer this worship in the traditional manner. A servant accompanied me. After reaching the end of the village, I stopped and asked the servant*

*if he knew the names of all the gods and goddesses. The servant replied that he only knew the name of some of the deities. "Well then," I said, "please let us invoke them with all our hearts and request them to come here to this place." Thus I lit all the candles at this place and broke all the coconuts right there.*

*The servant was shocked and tried to persuade me to go to every single deity and invoke their blessing. But my mind had a different make-up and usually did exactly whatever idea entered into my head — no matter how unusual. When I returned within a short time with all the coconuts cracked, I declared openly that I had requested all the deities to come to one place and they were offered the coconuts all together. My mother was annoyed but I took two or three coconut pieces and ran away to play with firecrackers.*

*The second incident was during a fair at Bangoli near Raipur (Chattisgarh, India). It was considered to be the biggest fair in the region. In front of the Shiva temple, I noticed that devotees were offering food and coins to the sadhus (monks) and not to the beggars. These sadhus were hale and hearty and had adopted some unusual ritualistic techniques to attract attention and funds. Some were keeping their head underneath the soil; others were sleeping on thorns in the hot sun, etc. Thus the people had developed reverence towards them and offered them coins. I surveyed all of this, climbed onto the platform, caught hold of three sadhus and made them stand. With a swelling throat, I told the people, "You see, they are hypocrites. They are exploiters. They possess no power and that is why they have come to exploit you. Better give your money to the beggars and the needy."*

*Then I collected all the coins from these sadhus and distributed them to the beggars. When I left that place, a thought struck my mind. Why had I just done all this? How did it happen all of a sudden? I could not make out the reason behind this indecorous behavior of mine. When this was reported to my mother, she simply said, "He is an atheist and will bring misfortune to my home."*

So recalling these two incidents I was at the hostel. At that time, all the boys were busy in daily routines as per the usual schedule. That evening, the majority of the boys had become ready to listen to the acarya, the stranger. I first sat with a heavy mind and then went to my room to sleep. I wanted to utilize the nighttime for studying.

Suddenly, at around 10:30 PM, I felt a sudden jerk and got up and moved outside, towards the gate — unmindful of the acarya's lecture. Almost everyone was there to see the yogi off. To my surprise, the departing acarya put his right hand on my head and said calmly that he would come tomorrow and give me initiation into yoga. From his hands on my head, sweet soothing vibrations ran down my spine. This was natural, after all, because the time of initiation had come.

I went back to my room and washed myself to become fresh so that I could study. I sat down on my bed to study through the night. The picture of that bearded acarya appeared in my mental plate. I fell soundly asleep and was floating the whole night in an ocean of sweet dreams.

Unlike on other days, I arose late, too late in fact. Nervously I performed my morning duties quickly. The very moment I was all ready, the yogi entered into the room. I greeted him with reverence by bowing my head.

"The time has come for initiation, you must get ready," he said.

I nodded my head affirmatively and said, "Yes I am ready."

And then the initiation was soon over. What a little boy like me could have comprehended out of all of this was certainly not even one percent. After initiation Acarya Ramshwarthiji asked me to do meditation in another room because there were a few more boys waiting for initiation.

I sat in sadhana for a full one and a half hours. Finally, my roommates interrupted to tell me that it was time for classes to start and that I should get ready. Almost everyone had already finished breakfast. When I went to the acarya's room to pay my respects and thanks, I found that he had already left. I was not to meet him again until 1963 when I became a monk myself and met him also as a monk named Acarya Shivananda Avadhuta.

Here what I want to say is that I became very restless. The reason is obvious, for I was blessed right at that first sitting with a link

between many past lives and the present. I was hankering after the sweet state of mind that I felt during initiation. With eyes half closed, the whole day I could not devote my mind to what the teachers were saying. I came back early and closed the door and sat in sadhana. It was a journey into an unknown world — a world I had been searching for many births.

Out of twelve boys initiated, I alone was particular about sadhana. After sadhana, I would go onto the roof and gaze at the stars and the moon. I was searching for something — known internally but still unknown.

At midnight, my roommates would ask, "What is wrong with you, my dear?"

# FIRST DARSHAN

Actually from the very first day of initiation, I continued my sadhana and remarkable changes took place within me. I used to walk for three kilometers to attend a group meditation every Sunday called Dharmacakra (DC). A lot of discussion had been there among the persons who had learnt sadhana and we used to assemble together in Giita Bhavan where collective meditation was held. I used to be told about a person everyone called "Baba". One day, I was told that Baba was coming to Raipur on December 17, 1961. I strongly felt that I must see Him. I had developed great respect and inquisitiveness to meet Him. He is Guru and Baba. I hardly knew what guru meant, but I liked the word "Baba". After all, in my village the word "Baba" was used for Shiva. In DC, we had the pratiika (six-pointed star symbol of Ananda Marga) and not His photo. All this was not my concern, but what I liked very much was the love and care of three persons whom I had not met earlier. They were Acarya Narendra Dube, Acarya Kailash Chand Bhalla and Acarya Ram Lal Dani. Later I came to know that my own Professor Rama Lal Dani was also an Acarya.

The time at last was approaching when we would be meeting the One whom we had unknowingly been cherishing and longing for. During one DC, it was announced that Baba was coming to Raipur and would stay for two days. The word "Baba" had by that time, become so endearing that it appeared to be my very own.

Then, finally the day of "Personal Contact" came. I myself had not gone to the railway station to receive the Person so much sought after by all. Then I went to the Gujarati Dharmsala (pilgrims inn) where all the programmes and the stay of this "Great Person" were arranged. The gathering of margis in the Dharmsala was charged with spiritual vibrations. One elderly man—who I later learnt was Acarya Hari Dutt, a member of the Paramilitary Forces — was be-

having very unusually as if in a state of delirium. I was told it was the result of his spiritual practice. To my surprise, I saw my margi friends there but no person in the dress of a sannyasi. But actually I was hardly interested in this. My curiosity was just to meet and see who this "Great Man" was. By then, everyone was shouting and there was a line at the gate before Baba's room.

Someone asked me to get in line. I just stood and met one person. He was Jageshwar (Acarya Acintananda Avadhuta). I don't remember now who was first in line — he or I. We were both initiated into the kingdom of love with Baba on the same day as far as I can recall.

As briefed, I entered His room and prostrated (sastaunga pranama). Baba was sitting on the cot. He signaled me to come closer to Him.

He said, "Get up my little boy." I got up and sat near the cot.

Then Baba said, "I have been wishing for many years to bring you, but there was no medium to do this."

Then Baba asked, "What is your name?"

"Pardeshi," I replied.

"Do you realize that three times you were destined to die in this very life and all the three times I saved you?" Baba then narrated all these incidents, one after another. He described it so vividly that it was as if I was seeing them with my own eyes.

I started crying. Baba took me upon His lap. How long He was stroking me with love as I was crying on His lap, I just do not know. We were together and I was crying, crying and crying. Maybe I was with Him for more than one hour.

When I was about to leave the room with eyes full of tears, Baba asked me for something very precious. Baba said, "Since I have saved your life, this life of yours belongs to me. Can you sacrifice this life for me?"

"Yes, Baba," I replied.

He asked me to place my hands on my chest and take an oath to work for Him. I took the oath in a state of elation. When I came out of the room, I was sobbing, overwhelmed by the majesty and intimacy of Baba's Darshan.

Later I thought about those three incidents. I was the youngest in the family. The railway line went near our village. It so happened one day that my elder brother and sister, without paying any heed to the oncoming danger, crossed the railway tracks and left me just between the tracks. I began crying as the train was coming, blowing its whistle. Someone picked me up and threw me outside the tracks. This hurt me a lot as it injured me on my waist where someone had picked me up and thrown me out.

The second incident was when I fell from a tree onto the ground like I was dead. Someone made me sit and revived my breathing. I felt someone doing this. But there was no one whom I could see.

The third incident was after the first day of my being admitted into school. I was running back across the railway lines to my village, when I tripped over the railway line and fell down breaking my hand. Someone quickly pulled me by my leg off the track. Who did it? It was none other than my Baba.

I had never thought that I would be in such a state of mind where I was completely different from my usual self. I was in a mysterious world. My revered Baba left Raipur but not me.

Like any other boy who had been sent to the city from the village by their parents, I was particular in devoting much time to study, for I had been sent to study, get a job and start earning. But now I was lost in Baba's love. Instead of sitting in the front bench, I shifted to the last and all the time kept my head on the desk.

"What has happened to you, Mr. Naik," asked the lecturer.

"Nothing, sir," was my prompt reply.

When this was reported to my parents, they took me back to the village. The worst affected was my mother. She wept and wept. Despite all this, I appeared in the annual examination and passed with distinction. I spent time with Jageshwar, who was staying just near my hostel. He became my mentor in the realm of Ananda

Marga and PROUT activities. PROUT is a new social and economic theory to replace capitalism and communism.

We both attended three VSS (Volunteer Social Service) camps in Rajim, Mana and Jabalpur. We students under the leadership of brother Jageshwar collected funds during the Chinese invasion of India in 1962. The funds were given to the Collector (District administrative and revenue head) in the name of the UPSF (Universal Proutists Students Federation).

Within a span of one and a half years, we organized a National Prout Convention in Raipur. A good number of youth joined Ananda Marga. In my village, regular DC was held and a sizeable amount of people joined Ananda Marga. I organized a farmers meeting in my village and Dada Abhedananda Avadhuta came and addressed the assembled farmers. The word 'Dada' means "elder brother" and is a term used in Ananda Marga for monks.

Life was full of joy and work. My Baba again came to Raipur the next year on October 10. At that time, none of us thought that we would become an acarya (teacher of spiritual meditation).

# CARING FOR A NEW WORKER

Although Acarya Ramshwarathji had initiated me, I had not met him again in my student life. I came in contact with one avadhuta in 1962 named Acarya Dhyanananda Avadhuta in Raipur. This was the first time I had met a monk of Ananda Marga dressed in orange.

It was one year later on 27 October 1963 during the Jabalpur DMC, when we eighteen boys left for Ananda Nagar (organizational headquarters) to join the ranks of renunciate monks (sannyasis). Jageshvar (Acarya Acintananda), Bhimrao (Acarya Asheshananda), Narayan (Acarya Narottamananda), Nagaraj (Vimalananda) and many others joined the caravan.

When we reached Ananda Nagar, we met Acarya Satyananda Avadhuta and Acarya Sujit Kumar. We stayed in a mud house with a thatched roof, near the Ananda Marga Institute of Technology (AMIT).

After our one-month stay at Ananda Nagar, only three of us trainees were sent to Jamalpur. After reaching there, I was more or less regularly getting the chance to see Baba alone in His room in the jagriti. A jagriti is a spiritual place of Ananda Marga for collective meditation.

Sometimes we would go out to see the town of Jamalpur, in whose lap Baba grew up. It was full of dust and dirt. The lanes and narrow roads were in fact reeked with foul smells coming from waste dumped on both sides. Here in this small town, Baba founded Ananda Marga. The most remarkable places to visit were Death Valley and the field beyond the railway offices, guesthouses and training institutes. We three trainees visited these places during the daytime. As it was said that Death Valley was dangerous due to tigers and other wild animals, we did not venture inside the forest. We once went up to the gate of Baba's office, but did not enter inside,

as entry was restricted. For food we were asked to go to a nearby hotel meant for margiis. Although the comforts and amenities were minimal, oh how we enjoyed our stay in Baba's hometown. Baba used to come every morning at around 7 a.m. Acarya Abhedananda Avt. (future martyr or dadhici) was in charge of the jagriti and taking care of Baba's personal needs.

After a few weeks I was asked to go to Muzaffarpur for my first posting. I stayed with Acarya Candranathji. I was very comfortable and enjoyed the company, affection and care of all family members. I was too young to understand the spiritual significance of Baba putting me in such spiritual company. I visited Acarya Sakal Deoji and Acarya Gangasharanji. Both of them were kapalikas and I had great respect for them. They also loved me very much. My worldly brother came there to take me back home. Both the Acaryas convinced him not to and he returned home. Later, I came to know from Baba when I met him after a few days, that Baba had asked those two acaryas to take special care of me. At the time, I was unable to understand why Baba did this.

Thereafter I was told to take some training under Acarya Prakashananda Avt. In late November 1963, there was a Dharma Maha Cakra (DMC) in Motihari. DMC was a special sacred event in which Baba would bless the margis not only with His presence but also with His Varabhaya Mudra or special gesture of blessing that was "radioactive" with spiritual energy. I was sitting in a room with many margis whom I had not met earlier. It was quite natural for me since I was too young and new to understand the situation. One or two margis felt happy that I had become an Ananda Marga sannyasi (renunciate worker for humanity). At that time becoming a wholetimer was considered to be a holy event. I occupied a special place in their minds and hearts. Everyone took special care and gave me full attention.

Then Baba suddenly entered the room. A chair was offered and He sat down. The whole room was charged with blissful energies. Looking at me, Baba asked me to stand up. Then addressing the small gathering in the room, He changed my name to Vishvabandhu. He then asked Dada Prakashananda to take special care of me. I was posted as a worker under his supervision. Later in the evening discourse, Baba explained in detail the meaning of the word *vishvabandhu*. Baba said that a person who cannot remain separate from Parama Purusa is known as *bandhu*. So the person who while in contact with the countless beings of the entire universe still never remains apart or separate from Parama Purusa is Vishvabandhu. I felt that the entire day had been devoted to me. I felt so exalted.

Baba then left for Jamalpur and I was traveling with Dada Prakashananda. Then came another celebration of joy when we reached Jamalpur after a few days and I could again see my Baba. I felt that if heaven did indeed exist then it was truly here with Baba.

# BABA LOVES THE WORD “BABA”

This is a sweet memory of 1963 in Jamalpur. It was evident to many people in Jamalpur that every day after sunset Baba would go to the tiger’s grave for evening walk. He used to sit there for some time and then come back. While going, four devotees used to accompany him. This was one group. In the second group there were another four persons who used to sit on the grave with Baba. The third group of another four people used to walk back home with Baba. It was a regular routine. There was a struggle among the devotees to join one of the groups. If a person’s name did not figure one day then he used to enroll himself for another day and then wait for this moment with anxiety. This arrangement was done under supervision of Acharya Abhedananda Avadhuta. Who will not want the company of Baba? Whether He was giving spiritual knowledge or worldly knowledge or especially when He was silent, Oh, how much bliss was drenching all the devotees’ minds. All three realms (material, psychic and spiritual) seemed to be totally connected and fused in one straight line emanating from Baba’s Feet. This bliss, the bliss of a young monk, is expressed so simply in one of His songs,

> ***DVAETÁDVAETA KONO JINÁN JE NÁHI ÁMÁR***
> ***JÁNI SHUDHU O DUT́I CARAŃ ÁCHE* (1228)**
>
> **I do not have any knowledge**
> **Of duality or non-duality.**
> **I only know**
> **That those two Feet of Yours**
> **Are there for me.**

The state of Bihar was new to me. It was December and January

with severe cold weather. I had no sweater and no blanket, as I did not feel them to be required. I was so happy to be in Jamalpur. I was blessed with so many chances to be with Baba, alone in His room in the jagriti. Acarya Abhedanandji told me to take my breakfast, lunch and dinner at a nearby tiny hotel run by a margi. I was so very happy to hear all these details of how I was going to stay with my Baba.

It was then that my father along with my uncle came to take me back home. When they saw Baba, they were very gratified. Later when I was alone with Baba, I told about how my father came and asked me to go back and see my mother at least once.

Baba said, “You go and come back after convincing your mother.”

When I reported this to Satyananda Dada, he did not agree to this idea and discussed this with my father. Then Acarya Pranayda, the general secretary, also talked to my father and convinced him to return back to Raipur. After seeing Baba and experiencing the spiritual atmosphere in the jagriti, he had developed reverence for Baba.

Senior acarya training took place in December and one day Baba made a rule that those who were attending senior acarya training would not join the three groups for field walk in the evening. That same day in the jagriti (meditation hall), Abhedananda Dada called me. He told me that Baba had said that in all three evening groups for field walk only Vishvabandhu would go. I was going to be alone with my Baba for the entire evening.

When Abhedananda Dada gave me this good news, I was happy like anything. So unbelievably happy that you cannot even imagine it. Previously also I had such a sweet opportunity for field walk with Him in one of the groups. But this time Baba and I were the only group. My feet were not even on ground and I felt like I was floating in a sky of bliss. Here I should clarify one more thing. Due to other sadhakas being present in the field walk groups, there was some mental inhibition in me when speaking to Baba.

Abhedananda Dada called me and gave me instructions to carry a bedsheet, a thermos filled with water, one glass tumbler and a torch, which were required for visiting the field. Dada showed me where everything was kept. I was supposed to remember to take all these things along with me while going with Baba. When he was sure that I had understood everything, he kept them in a room packed in a box.

I reached Baba’s quarters before the scheduled time and after waiting for some time Baba came out. He came near to me and lovingly said, “Vishvabandhu. You have come.”

Then He touched my head and stroked my back as if He knew something that I could not understand. The same sweet touch with which I used to be blessed in the jagriti every morning. Many times, feeling that touch we were as ONE. Radha-Krsna and Krsna-Radha. What happened to me I don’t know. I was overwhelmed with the feeling that I would be with Baba for two whole hours.

I replied, “Yes Baba.”

“Let us move.”

I then started walking with Baba. Everyone was aware of Baba’s tremendous speed while walking, such that many times one would have to run to keep up with Him. After walking for some distance the path passed by a tree. Baba kept some distance from the tree as He walked and keeping His hand on my shoulder He told me to walk in the same manner. After passing the tree, He again came onto the main road. I was anxious to know why Baba bypassed the tree. Later on I came to know the birds in the tree would dirty the clothes of those who passed below and that was why Baba changed our route.

We both walked and walked ... crossed the railway bridge ... then one narrow road and another narrow road. Then we came to the end of the road and the field started. Regarding what we talked I only remember one thing. There were many mango trees by the roadside. Baba asked me what the word for mango in Chattisgarhi was. I replied, “*aam*.”

“No, *aamaa*,” He said correcting me.

“Yes, Baba.”

The moment we reached the field, I realized that I had made a mistake. It was completely dark and I could hardly see anything, not even the footpath leading to the tiger’s grave where Baba used to sit. I felt secure however. Why not? After all, I was alone with Baba in that silent valley.

I said, “Baba, I have forgotten to bring the torch and other things. Dada Abhedananda had told me to carry it but I left it all behind!”

“Don’t worry,” Baba replied. “I am here.” Then He caught my hand and we walked on.

What a heaven I was in. We walked with my hand in Baba’s. Then I would embrace Him and then again we would walk ... and so on for a brief eternity. We reached the tiger’s grave. I was very sad, for I had not brought the cotton sheet to spread for Him to sit on. Then Baba Himself sat down and asked me to sit. I was overwhelmed with reverence and tried to sit down on the ground in front of Him.

But Baba said, “Not there, sit here,” indicating a spot beside Him. I just sat near His feet. I started massaging His feet. This I had never, ever done before for anyone in my entire life. O how happy I was all alone with Baba.

Suddenly Baba asked, “Would you like to learn kapalika sadhana?”

“Yes.”

“Now!”

“As you wish, Baba.”

“What is your wish?” Baba asked.

I replied, “Whatever you wish, Baba.”

Baba asked me three times and all three times I replied, “As you wish, Baba.”

Baba asked me, “Will you not be afraid?”

“No, Baba. When you are with me, why should I be afraid?”

After a pause, Baba said, “Can you climb that tree?”

“Yes, Baba,” I replied.

“All right, climb it.”

I left the tiger’s grave to go towards the tree. When I had hardly taken three steps, suddenly I could not move further. I couldn’t see Baba as well. I was in a fix. What to do? I sat in sadhana.

Baba came and said, “Oh, you are just sitting here.” Then Baba took me back to the tiger’s grave once again. We were together, all alone in the vast world under the open night sky.

I said, “Baba?” He remained silent. Again I called Him, “Baba,” but He remained silent. Then, three or four times I called out, “Baba”.

The next time, I said with a loud exclamation, “Baba!” shaking His feet.

“Say what you want.” But I simply could not recall the question I had in my mind.

Then I said, “Baba, I called Your Name several times. Why did You not reply?”

Then Baba replied, “I feel very pleased when you say ‘Baba’. Had I replied to you the first time, you would not have said ‘Baba ... Baba ... Baba’ repeatedly.”

Baba then paused. The whole valley was steeped in absolute silence. No sound could be heard in any direction.

Suddenly we both noticed that some persons were standing at a distance. Baba shouted, “Who is there?”

"Prakashananda, Baba," came the reply.

"Why are you here?"

"Baba, Vishvabandhu has forgotten to bring the bedsheet and other required items and it is a dark night, so we brought them." "Let us go then," Baba said.

We went back to the jagriti after leaving Baba at His quarters.

Prakashananda and the others said, "Vishvabandhu, we have decided to escort you to do night sadhana."

Realizing they were referring to kapalika sadhana, I said, "Baba did not teach me."

"Did He mention about this sadhana?"

"Yes," I replied, narrating the entire incident.

"You are the biggest fool. You should have said, 'Yes, Baba, I am ready.' You have missed a good chance. Whenever Baba takes anyone alone like this, He teaches him kapalika sadhana. We had discussed this among ourselves and decided one of us would accompany you to the cremation ground. But you turned out to be a fool."

The next morning, when Baba came to the jagriti, I was blessed with the chance to be alone with Him. I said, "Baba, teach me kapalika sadhana."

Baba replied, "You told me it is my wish. So not now."

I felt sad that I had missed out on the opportunity to get kapalika sadhana at that time. On two or three other occasions when I had the chance to request Baba again to learn this sadhana, His reply was the same.

The next day, Acarya Gangasharan from Muzzaffarpur, who was attending the senior acarya training, came up to me and asked me to accompany him to the market in Jamalpur. Upon my asking why he wanted me to come, he said that Baba had asked him to buy a sweater for me. I felt so thrilled to be cared for like this. Then I remembered the previous evening when I was standing in front of Baba's quarters without any warm clothes, Baba had stroked my head and back. I felt cared for and protected. You cannot imagine how the simplest act of kindness from Baba was so full of loving concern that it would make one so happy that tears would begin to fill the eyes. And I was no longer sad at having missed learning kapalika sadhana.

During senior acarya training on Sunday, Baba came to the jagriti. After some required organizational work, He summoned me to His room. He asked me a question. "Do you know the ten vayus (vital airs of the body)?"

I felt clearly that I was sitting in an examination being conducted by Baba. I replied that I knew them. I had attended camps in Raipur and training in Ananda Nagar where these were taught.

Then Baba asked, "Do you know about PROUT?"

"Yes, Baba," I replied.

"Can you teach Me also?"

That was indeed a serious question for me. I began to introspect about what answer I should give. Acarya Arun (Svarupananda) was standing beside Baba. He nodded his head, indicating, "Yes." So I said, "Yes, Baba."

"How many lessons have you learned?"

"Three, Baba."

Baba then asked Acarya Gangasharanji to complete all my lessons in the next room. Dada taught me in a short time. Then I was again called into Baba's room.

What a great blessing! Revered Baba put both His legs down and said, "Touch My feet and take the acarya oath." Touching His sweet

feet and repeating after Baba, I took the oaths of an Acarya of His Ananda Marga.

Thus I became an Acarya on January 17, 1964.

> **Aananda Maarga Certificate**
>
> I hereby authorise Shri Vishva Bandhu
> Vill- Radwa, P.O.- Silyari, Dist- Raipur (M.P.)
> Acarya — 17.1.1964
> Marga Guru: Anandamurti
> Registered S.L. No. 227, Date: 28.5.1964

# HE IS EVERYWHERE

On the 18 of January 1964, we were three newly ordained Acaryas sitting in Baba's room in the Jamalpur jagriti. Baba gave a brief inspiring talk, of which I did not understand much because my mind was absorbed in Him. What I could know was the blessing that He gave.

He assured us that He would always be with us. We should not be afraid of anything. I was told to work in Punjab. At that time present-day Haryana was also part of Punjab.

Before I left the room, He told me to go to Shrii Manohar Lal Agarwal, a local Acarya of Ananda Marga at Rohtak, and seek his guidance. We did Sastaunga Pranama (full prostration) and left.

I was asked to go on the same day, along with Acarya Anathabandhuji. He was posted in Rajasthan and myself in undivided Punjab. We boarded the Upper India Express and reached Delhi well in time. I headed towards Rohtak and Acaryaji proceeded to Jaipur by bus. I stayed with Acarya Manohar Lal comfortably for a week. Upon my request, he chalked out my programme for Punjab. First I was to go to Ambala city, then Chandigarh, next Pathankot, after that Ludhiana, on to Panipat, then to Rewari and finally come back to Rohtak after twenty-seven days. After getting some information and some money for expenses, I left for Ambala by a local train. My mind started painting vivid pictures of Ambala city, a place that I had never seen and was going to all alone. Where to stay and whom to contact were the paramount questions haunting my mind. We were told in Jamalpur that if there was no jagriti in any place, we should stay in a dharmshala (pilgrims inn). To my great relief, Acaryaji had given me information about Shrii Moolchanda Raijada, whose address he himself did not know. He simply told me that people knew him and so he could be easily located.

With the puffing of the steam engine of the train, my heart started

puffing also. I was travelling alone. My mind went back through the events of earlier days.

I, like many other village boys, had never travelled beyond the district town during my days of education, except once when I accompanied many others to another district town to attend the VSS (Volunteer Social Service) camp. I was a junior in a group. After getting PC with Baba some twenty days after initiation, the door of my mind was opened a little wider as I attended three camps. When we left our home state and joined Him fully we were eighteen in number. My mind had nourished the idea to be with Baba all the time. I left everything behind — love, care, career, parental affection — and accepted Baba as my be-all and end-all. It was so sudden and spontaneous that I could not understand it. I had no need to understand. I was too small to grasp the meaning of "Tattvika" (one who knows Ananda Marga philosophy; one who is established in the absolute reality), "Acarya" (or one who teaches practical spirituality by his/her own conduct) or "Sannyasi" (someone who has completely renounced body, mind and soul and fully surrendered to the Lord and the ideology).

The sole idea I was absorbed in was that I would be with Baba all the time. And that was all in all with me. During two and a half months of my stay in late 1963 in Jamalpur, Bihar, He blessed me a number of times — almost every day — with His personal touch and personal contact. To tell the truth, during this period in which He entered into my life, the more I came closer, the more I was lost. I was His and He was mine. He was always with me physically, mentally and spiritually.

When Baba personally sent me to Muzaffarpur under the care of Acarya Candranathji, I found myself surrounded by him, his wife (who was also an Acarya), Acarya Sakal Deoji and Acarya Ganga Sharanji. This was still a most painful time, being separated from Baba for the first time. But there also, I was not alone. When I was ordained as an Acarya by Baba, I had hardly understood its significance and I was so happy, but when I came to know that I would go to the field away from Baba, the pangs of anxiety, sadness and fear gripped my mind. Knowing full well my state of mind, Baba had given such an inspiring and touching talk in His room before I left for my posting.

In any case, until my last hours in Rohtak, I was not really worried for the simple reason that I was always in the company of either Acaryaji or Baba. I had never remained alone in the past. Therefore when I left Rohtak the feeling of loneliness started haunting me. I felt that I was away from Baba completely uncared for, unprotected. The more I looked at the other passengers, the more I developed a feeling of being lost. I wept many times, rather throughout the whole journey. Baba was in my heart and my heart was lost in a dark void. I moved along with the train. That six-hour journey was the longest journey I ever undertook in my life.

In the train I was horrified to see many Punjabis. To me Punjabis were Sikhs only, and coming from a Hindu background, I had learnt many prejudices about Sikhs. In fact, I had never seen them before — certainly not in such a large number. What I had been told about them was that they were rash, rude, harsh, aggressive and unthinking followers of orders. To confirm my convictions, I found those on the train (not everyone though) in a drunken state and behaving abnormally. I could not do sadhana not only because they were behaving in a somewhat bullying manner towards me, but because I was absorbed in a state of fear psychosis. I was praying to Baba to come to my rescue.

By the time the train reached Ambala city, it was already 10 p.m. It was completely dark and drizzling. The climate was extremely cold. I collected my luggage, which was no more than a bundle of clothes and some of Baba's books wrapped in blankets, and moved towards the gate. Since it was raining, I stopped for a while in the platform shade. I noticed that all had left. I avoided the platform, in fear of being robbed by those Sikhs or being left dead, frozen by the hands of the chilling cold. When a Sikh shouted at me my thought vacillated between life and death. It was all in Punjabi, which I did not understand.

For the first time in my life, a Sikh ticket collector confronted me. He brought my luggage down and laying a stick on it he commanded me to open it. He wanted to see what it contained. He abused me at least five times. It was all very unpleasant, harsh and rough. I closed my eyes and prayed to Baba for rescue. When he found nothing that he expected, he directed me towards the gate.

I came out with my mind completely spaced out. And what awaited

me next? There was only one rickshaw standing in a vast, open stand. All had left. He came to me and enquired in Punjabi. I found him also to be a Sikh. I could not understand anything at all of what he said. Then he spoke in Hindi. I felt a bit closer and relieved. By this time I was fully drenched in rain. I told him that I wanted to go to Moolchand Raijada's house. The promptness of his reply that he knew him and the way he took my luggage on the rickshaw and helped me up to sit further aggravated my fear.

My readers, you can imagine what the state of my mind would have been. I was being drawn by a man who was none other than a Sikh — a robber in the dark night to take me into the jaws of death. My prejudices were growing as fast as my fears. My heart was struck thinking that this man must have lied when he said he knew Raijadaji's house and undoubtedly had hatched a vicious plan to rob me. There was none with me — no margis, no acaryas, not even Baba physically. I had instinctively surrendered myself to Baba and it was my most complete surrender in my life thus far.

That unknown rickshaw driver wanted to know whether I was coming to Punjab for the first time and so many other things. As time went by, from the way he talked I began to like him. "Crich.." the rickshaw stopped. "Here is the place you want," the rickshaw puller said before he himself knocked on the door.

One man peeped out from the first floor window and enquired who I was. I gave the reference of Acaryaji. He came down with a torch in hand and opened the door. The rickshaw man took my luggage down inside the gate and said, "Now you are safe." I was so happy with that gentleman that I paid a note of two rupees presumably much more than the actual fare. I thanked him and started collecting my luggage. He asked me to stop and to my surprise gave me back one rupee and fifty paisa with the explanation that he was a Sikh and hence could not take more than what the fare was. I developed a high regard for that driver — the Sikh.

I stayed there for four days and during my tour of twenty-five days in Punjab in which I covered all areas assigned, I initiated quite a good number of persons, including Sikhs, in meditation. My mind was always high. My feelings about Sikhs changed completely. I came back to Rohtak as scheduled. I was handed over a telegram sent by Acarya Pranay Dada — the general secretary of Ananda Marga. Its contents were simple — "Reach Jamalpur immediately." I got myself ready and headed with galloping speed towards Jamalpur — the abode of my Baba, my Lord. Why not, after all? I had achieved a lot. I had made many contacts, many group discussions and many initiations. I was going with a crown of success upon my head and would tell Baba all about this.

My journey from Rohtak to Jamalpur was a journey of joy, achievement, success and many fantasies, especially the idea as to when I would be alone with Baba... on His lap... and narrate the whole story of how I did this and that, and so on and so forth.

I reached Jamalpur and became ready by 7 a.m., much ahead of the usual time of Baba's regular visit to the jagriti. When Baba came I was standing first in the row to welcome Him. My heart was jumping with joy. When He came near me, He smiled and pinched my cheek with His right hand. He radiated a blissful light from His twinkling eyes and then left. Baba entered His room, followed by the great Acarya Abhedanandaji, His PA. Then dada came out and asked me to go inside.

I entered with high emotions, prostrated fully and hurriedly got up and went to Baba's lap straightaway. I was fully lost. When I came to my senses a little, Baba asked me about whether I had faced any difficulties in Punjab.

I said, "No, Baba!" Baba did not give me any chance to tell Him all about my achievements. Instead, He started narrating the whole sequence of my journey from Rohtak to Ambala city, how I was afraid, how I was shouted at by one Sikh, and how the rickshaw puller, another Sikh, came to my rescue.

And then... and then He took a fifty-paisa coin from His pocket and keeping it in my hand, said, "This is the money you gave the rickshaw-puller; you take it."

That was more than enough for me to realize everything. I clung to Baba like a baby to his mother. I started weeping. The tears started rolling down my cheeks with Baba rubbing them with His own hand.

I was weeping and am still weeping thinking about it. How long I wept and how long I will weep — I don't know.

# SEVA DHARMA MISSION

In 1964, Seva Dharma Mission (SDM) was newly formed as an organization. It arose out of historical necessity. Shrii Prabhat Ranjan Sarkar (Baba) was the president of Ananda Marga Pracaraka Samgha, which during 1955 was registered in Bihar under the Bihar Societies Act. There were a few more office bearers. What prompted the creation of a separate organization named SDM was a criminal case filed by the father of a boy from Bihar against Baba. The boy was none other than Acarya Amitananda Avadhuta. He was a brilliant young engineering graduate from Bihar who became a monk of Ananda Marga. The father, a well-to-do officer working in the Bihar State Government filed a kidnapping case against Baba. To forestall this, Amitanandaji was told to join a popular organization in Varanasi known as Bharat Seva Sangh (BSS) and reside in their ashram. When he was produced in court, he stated that he was a disciple of Shrii Shrii Anandamurtiji but was not a worker of AMPS. Although Lord Anandamurtiji gave him a sannyasi name, he was a registered worker of the BSS in Varanasi. He was then set free.

Hence the need arose then for another organization named SDM with headquarters in Varanasi. This was but another pretext for Baba to manifest more aspects of His mission, which He envisioned in His early childhood in his ancestral home of Bamunpara (Barddhaman District). SDM was first given the mission of fighting communalism (religious extremism), casteism and other divisive dogmas in the society. It was a man-making mission based on spiritual meditation, universal outlook and fostering the dormant potentialities in every community and every individual. The main mission of course was the promotion of yoga research and the mass propagation of yoga to the masses free of cost and free from any religious, national and other narrow sentiments.

A new system was introduced: All new aspiring monks and nuns of Ananda Marga had to give a declaration in writing that they were of legal age and had joined SDM out of their own free will. This declaration had to be sent to the parents, head of police and the administrative authority of their home district. Thus Ananda Marga was free from any legal hurdles from parents. SDM was then in charge of imparting basic training for monks and nuns becoming tattvikas and acaryas and the training centre was known as Prashikśańa Matha.

# HE NEVER SUPPOSES

Some days after arriving in Jamalpur from Punjab, Baba said that now I was to go to Himachal Pradesh as CO (Circle Organizer) with my headquarters in Simla. We were only a few workers at that time in India and those who were in charge of a province were known as COs. In Punjab I was working under the guidance and supervision of Ac. Omkarananda Avt.. He was the CO of Punjab and Ac Parashanath was the CO of the province of Jammu & Kashmir. Sometimes Acarya Amresh visited Himachal Pradesh as a family acarya to do pracar. All three were initiated into kapalika sadhana — a special tantric night meditation performed in the cremation or burial ground. They were all close friends.

My Baba had asked me to work in Himachal Pradesh as CO. He gave me special instructions and assured me that He would be with me at all times. After prostrating myself before Him, I left the room. Acarya Abhedananda Avadhuta, Baba's personal assistant, entered where I was and after a while told me that Baba wanted me to come to His room again.

Dearest Baba said that on the way to Himachal, I should get down at the city of Varanasi and give a letter to Ac. Prakashananda Avt., who was in charge of our newly opened training centre for sannyasis. It was about the transfer of one of the trainees from the centre. I could guess that it would cause trouble for the training centre and that legal complications could arise. This was because when idealistic young men decided to become monks, often their parents would try to kidnap them or file cases claiming they were kidnapped. Some parents would even try to harm our Baba physically.

The case concerned Acarya Amitananda Avadhuta who was later posted as the first Rector of Ananda Nagar and the first principal of AMIT (Ananda Marga Institute of Technology). He was very intelligent and his memory was very sharp. He was the only one among us who could repeat Baba's discourses word for word. His love for Baba was such that once when Baba's visit to Ananda Nagar was planned and Baba changed His programme and did not come, Amitanandji was most offended and in an accusing tone scolded Baba while He listened in silence. Amitanandji died due to prolonged illness due to overworking. Thereafter, whenever Baba came to Ananda Nagar, Baba would say how He was feeling the absence of Amitanandji.

Similar was the case with R. Prasad in Dei. Baba said many times that whenever He came to Delhi, how He felt the absence of Raghuvir Prasad. What a bond of inseparable love they both had with Baba.

I was to deliver the message to Prakashananda Dada. There was no telephone in the ashram at that time. So I asked Baba that supposing this Dada was not present at the training centre, then to whom should I give this message.

Baba replied, "You are to go there and deliver."

I again asked, "Baba suppose he is not there, then?"

"Why do you say this word 'suppose' to Me?"

Revered Baba then said that He never supposes.

I kept quiet. I thought, "Yes, Parama Purusa (the Supreme Entity) never supposes, proposes, or preposes."

Later, when I was asked to go to Himachal Pradesh in early March, I was told by Acarya Satyananda to meet him at Pathankot so that we all could move together to Jammu for DMC.

# JAMMU DMC

It was 1964. I had gone to Jammu for DMC on the 26 of April. It was in fact the first DMC I was attending after becoming an acarya and being posted in Himachal Pradesh. As a new worker, I had many turbulent feelings in my heart. First I had toured Simla, Bilaspur, Mandi and Dharmsala for a full month. In Simla I did practically nothing except sitting and doing meditation most of the time. Since it was intensely cold, I remained in my room. Sometimes in the day when the sun was out, I used to move about. Nevertheless I had initiated a group of people. Similar was my experience in Bilaspur, Mandi and Dharmsala. I was so thrilled and excited when I received the news of the date of the coming DMC in Jammu.

On the way to Jammu, at Pathankot, we met Dada Satyananda Avadhuta, a towering personality who was the first monk of our Baba. He was accompanied by Acarya Shambhusharan, Acarya Om Prakash and Acarya Parashnath. Acarya Parashanath was in charge of the state of Jammu and Kashmir, Ac. Om Prakash was in Punjab and Ac. Shambhusharan was looking after the work of PFI (Proutist Forum of India) — an organization for the propagation of PROUT, the revolutionary socio-economic theory given by Baba to create a revolution in every sphere of life from agriculture to culture, from the economic to the social and political realms. We all moved onto the bus. All of us were in civil dress except Satyanandji as in those days new WTs were not prescribed a uniform nor were they addressed as brahmacarii. The term brahmacarii was introduced perhaps in 1968-9. We reached there two days before Baba's arrival. The time passed quickly until Baba arrived.

A lot of good and devoted margis of Jammu organized everything. Shri Kishanji, a margi who later became a family acarya, an advocate, a doctor and two boys, Sanjiva and Ranjiva, played stellar roles in organizing the DMC. More than four hundred devotees

were present. Venerable Baba seemed to be everywhere — in the kitchen, in our group discussions, in field walk, in General Darshan. He was the irresistible centre of attention at all times and His presence was felt everywhere by everyone. I was too young to take in all that was happening in this DMC Liila (divine events). Unlike DMCs after 1978, this DMC was just like being in a joint family on the move at all times.

Baba was there when margis were taking food. Baba would also come inside the kitchen and chit-chat with the sisters who were cooking. Such blissful visits Baba would pay, filling the sisters with sheer joy as they later prepared food soaked in the nectar of that joy. Wherever margis would sit and discuss, Baba would always be there listening so happily, gazing with such love at everyone and filling them with such bliss by making them all laugh and laugh at His jokes and then making them cry when He would give a devotee an extra special moment of His omnipotent tenderness. At that time, DMC truly was *Ek Chula, Ek Chaoka, Ek hae Manav Samaj* (One stove, one kitchen, one human society).

There were two rooms opposite each other and an open terrace between the two. Baba was staying in one and we wholetime acaryas in another. When Baba after morning walk started going up the staircase, I caught hold of Baba's left hand with my right hand. It was so natural, just like a small boy catching his father's hand. I then climbed up along with Baba. Then I requested Baba to grant PC to one new margi who had come from Himachal Pradesh, where I was posted.

When we both came to the top of the stairs, we entered together into the room, hand-in-hand. Baba then said, "Now you release My hand." I suddenly became conscious of what I had been doing. Baba many times used to suffer when people touched His body since their samskaras would come to Him. But here blindly, instinctively, I caught Baba's hand and Baba did not show anything. That time walking up the stairs was so short but perhaps it was the sweetest time in my life. An ordinary human boy like me just caught the hand of the Lord of the universe. To think about it makes the mind numb with wonder and the eyes flow with utmost gratitude. I wish we could have spent eternity on this planet, just walking on and on — hand in hand.

What happened next is beyond my understanding. I have told this story to many devotees so many times, in so many places. When Acarya Shambhusharan came into the room, Baba was sitting on His cot. We two workers were there. Baba was getting ready to give PC.

Baba asked me if would it look all right if this wooden cot were put on the other side of the room. Suddenly Baba got down and went to one side of the cot and lifted it with His hands. I rushed and took charge of the cot. Shambhusharan took the other side.

And then what next? I suddenly saw Baba. He was standing with both His hands raised a little in a gesture of blessing (Varabhaya Mudra). His face was full of divine lustre and effulgence (*ojas*) with an actual halo that was radiating light.

Shambhusharanji, who had not looked at Baba, shouted at me because I was not moving the cot. With a gesture of my head, I directed his attention towards Baba. He also saw Baba in all that spiritual glory.

After this spiritual blessing, known to Him alone but a great mystery to us, Baba asked us to move the cot a little forward. We did this but remained in utter silence, asking no questions.

Then Baba said, "You do not know, many persons come to me. They seek my advice and blessings. They are your big brothers. They are always ready to help you and are working for Dharma. It is still a mystery to me. I have narrated this story to many people but have yet to begin to fathom what happened."

What was a revelation was how Baba (with us unevolved disciples) has to diminish His spiritual effulgence so much. With more evolved sadhakas, He can reveal a little of His true nature. I still hope that one day I will be able to see my Baba with all His spiritual glory. Only He can elevate a mischievous person like myself, to be blessed with such an experience.

After this, Baba asked me to close the door and latch it from the inside. I was in His lap and we both embraced each other. We were both full of love. Then Baba lovingly said that He knew all the great difficulties that I faced during these past months in Himachal Pradesh.

"But," Baba said, "I was always with you; did you realize it?"

I replied, "Yes Baba."

Tears rolled down my cheeks and Baba started wiping them with His own hands. Even today, I still feel the tender warmth of those soft hands. We had been together for quite some time.

I opened the door. Shambhusharanji didn't like me having been in Baba's room for so long. It was nearing the time when many devotees would be coming for PC (Personal Contact with Baba) and Shambhusharanji was in charge of arrangement for PC.

At one time during this cherished DMC, Baba called me and told me, "Recite your Ista Mantra and get bliss." I tried this and deeply relished this bliss. This blessing from Baba is still with me to this very day.

# BIRTH OF OUR UNIFORM

It was possibly in May 1964, when we were in Jamalpur that a new dress was given to us similar to what Ananda Marga monks wear today except that it was more yellowish-orange in colour. At that time, those who were not yet avadhutas wore only the gown. Those who were in charge of one province were considered to be the CO taking charge of pracar (propagation of the ideology of Ananda Marga), opening of AMPS units and schools.

When all the COs of India, approximately thirteen to fourteen in number, put on their uniforms, Baba came out of His room in the Jamalpur Jagriti to see how we looked. He was very happy. This was the first introduction of the gown of our uniform. Sometimes we travelled in uniform and at other times in civil dress, as no prescribed rules were given at that time. We sometimes used to wear pants or dhoti, as only later was the white lungi prescribed as part of the uniform. At that time, the turban was compulsory for avadhutas only. As mentioned earlier, the term "brahmacarii" was introduced for non-avadhutas only much later in around 1968-9.

When I left Jamalpur for my new assignment as CO of Himachal Pradesh, I was strictly told that since I was a wholetime acarya, I should get instructions from Acarya Satyananda Avadhuta. Acarya Parashnath, Acarya Omprakash and Acarya Amresh were householder acaryas who had learned kapalika sadhana. They devoted their spare time for pracar. In that inspirational time in the history of Ananda Marga, these three householder acaryas took the entire land of northern India for their field of activities. Later Acarya Lalan joined them in this noble mission. Acarya Shambhusharan was a monk like me and was looking after PROUT work under the banner of PFI in Jammu and Kashmir. His duty was to publish the Urdu newspaper *Avaz-e-Jahan* from Jammu.

# LOVING EXPELLED DEVOTEE

On March 19, 1965, there was DMC at Ludhiana. The arrangement for Baba's stay was made in a newly constructed but still incomplete building. Those days, in some houses, the wooden toilet was in the bathroom. The system was such that the commode used to be manually cleaned and then only would it be fit to be re-used. The margi unit of Ludhiana was considered to be the best in the state. A good number of disciples were present from all walks of life.

As is usual, whenever and wherever there is a large number of persons in one place, some differences crop up. Dr Mahajan was declared a non-margi by the unit of Ludhiana for his alleged improprieties in personal life. He was not allowed into the DMC. Much earlier this doctor had gone to Jamalpur and seen Baba.

The morning after Baba arrived in Ludhiana, He entered His bathroom for morning duties. After a while, Baba's voice was heard from outside. Baba then opened the door. There was blood all over the floor and Baba kept the leg pressed with His finger. The wooden commode had broken and the nail from the commode had pierced Baba's leg and so blood was flowing out.

The margis decided to call the doctor. Baba said, "Where is My doctor Mahajan? Call him. He will take care of Me."

The margis had no option but to call the doctor whom they had expelled and declared as a non-margi. Just see, be the doctor a good boy or a bad boy, he was Baba's boy. Baba wanted to see His boy and for him to be part of His family gathering and receive His blessing. Baba knew if He asked for this supposedly "bad" man to come, perhaps the margis would have protested. So Baba created this drama and gave His blood to bring His son back into His family. This is how a real father behaves.

*Sadly this is a lesson many margis have refused to learn. People*

*have expelled each other, called others defector. They forget what Baba Himself wrote condemning ostracism (expulsion).*

**Ananda Marga keeps its gates open so that each and every man and woman may enter. Anybody may join, anybody may sing in chorus with the rest. The united march of the people is a march of victory. Ananda Marga states this in unequivocal language. ("Spirit of Society", A Few Problems Solved Part 6)**

**He is the Father of both developed sádhakas (yogis) and of sudurácáras or mahápátakiis (great sinners). There is, therefore, no question of His hating the sudurácáras (very sinful persons) and loving the good sádhakas. All are one to Him. He cannot ostracize the former and embrace the latter. There is no place outside Him where He can throw off the sudurácáras. He cannot disown them either, for in that case He would not remain *Patitpavan* (Saviour of the Degraded). ("Mysticism and Spirituality", *Subháśita Saḿgraha Part 19*)**

# SEE THE BRIGHT SIDE

The following incident took place during the Gazipur DMC in 1965. Baba stayed at Acharya Keshava's residence during the DMC. One day it happened that we were to offer orange juice to Baba. Unlike today there were no juice machines available. There was a plastic juice squeezer from which juice was made by revolving it. A few sweet limes (mosambi) were squeezed but still only half a glass of juice was produced. Now it was not possible to go to market to get more sweet limes, as Baba was ready at that time. Baba has nodded yes to receive the juice. The second issue was that it was possible that the half glass of juice would go sour in the heat. Acarya Keshava was in a dilemma what to do. Baba would have to be given only half a glass of juice, since it was not wise to mix water in it. I immediately said, "Give the half glass of juice to Baba since He knows everything. Baba will be happy."

Baba was waiting inside. Then Acharyaji offered the half glass of juice to Baba. Baba took the glass in his hand and said, "Keshava, I see only the part of the glass which is full. I am not looking at the empty part."

Baba drank juice and said, "You people should also look at good qualities of a person. You know, I also look at your good qualities. Suppose that you have nine bad qualities out of ten and only one good qualitity; I will try to increase your one good quality. From one to two, two to three, three to four, your noble qualities will continuously increase. And in the same proportion your bad qualities will decrease. From nine to eight, eight to seven, seven to six, likewise."

***

Baba was talking about Himachal Pradesh during one meeting with the disciples ... Suddenly he asked me about a margi, "How is he?" I was about to say something about him, when Baba suddenly said, "You have to walk too much. People are nice there. The people of these mountains are peace-loving and good by nature..."

When everyone left Baba's room, Baba said, "You wanted to say that he had left sadhana (meditation)."

I said, "Yes Baba."

Baba replied, "See, at that time some new disciples were present. If you had said something negative in front of them, such as that this person had left sadhana then they would have thought that sadhana can be abandoned and that anyone can leave sadhana. You should never express such negative feelings about anyone or about such happenings. So I changed the subject of conversation."

In such simple ways, He used to teach us how to live in this world.

# DUTY OF A SANNYASI

There was a DMC in Delhi. I was then working in Himachal Pradesh as Circle Organizer. I had gone there in advance to attend the DMC. We were few sannyasis. Baba was coming to Delhi by aeroplane. We went to welcome him at the airport.

At the same time an important dignitary was also coming from overseas. To welcome him, the President of India, Dr. Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan, also came to the airport. At that time the national and international airport were the same.

Maybe it happened that Baba's plane was a little late due to some reason. Fortunately many margis and sannyasis were present there. The President was sitting in his security conclave and was feeling a little inconvenience due to our presence. Suddenly a senior security officer asked about us. We told him everything. He went and told everything to the President. The whole environment was focused on Baba due to His arrival.

After coming inside, Baba sat with us in a semi-circle for some time. At the same time, the senior security officer came and told us that the President wanted to meet Baba. Some margis were considering this, when Baba left by car to the place where His stay was arranged.

When Baba was told about the President's wish He called Acharya Vimuktananda and told him to meet the President. Baba instructed him as follows: "When you go there, you must not sit on a chair. You will not take anything from him, not even water. Tell him only, 'You are the President of India and the situation of the country is serious; you are responsible for this.' Then inform him, 'if you want to meet Gurudeva then you have to first learn sadhana from an Acarya. You also have to regularly attend Dharmachakra. You have to follow Yama and Niyama — the cardinal principles of morality.'"

Dada went and repeated Baba's message and gave him the book

*Idea and Ideology* and then departed. Later, the President, who was a world-renowned professor of Indian philosophy, commented that this was a completely original philosophy. It is said that he wished to meet Baba, but Baba refused on the grounds of mistakes he made in political life.

***

Dadas who were working in various states of northern India had assembled in Delhi. During previous DMCs we used to attend with an easy heart and mind. We would chat with Baba so intimately that we hardly used to feel any tension. The entire atmosphere used to be homely. His presence was everywhere: in the rooms, verandah, kitchen and darshan hall. Practically, He was the presiding Lord everywhere. There was no pressure or tension of any kind, be it physical, economical or organizational. There was no fasting. There was no restriction on how many times per day we could eat and how many food items each time. We were not banned from eating in restaurants. There was no Sixteen Points or any restriction whatsoever. The relations among margis, among workers and between both were affectionate and blissful. Ours was a moving joint family.

My first RDS (Review Defects Solutions) was during this Delhi Dharma Maha Cakra. At that time I was standing in a queue of workers. As the reader will know by now, I would always try to sit near Baba. I was always longing to sit near Baba. If someone took the front place, I felt bitter. In this way, I used to try to reach as near to Baba as possible. This was my spiritual longing.

From this time onwards, whenever Baba would come to DMC, He first held RDS with the workers present. He examined all workers posted in their respective places, how successful those workers were in their required duties. If they were not successful, then the reasons why not, would have to be identified. No matter what the standard of success would have been in practical or mundane terms, Baba used to evaluate or review the workers' lives and work from the spiritual viewpoint. Baba would give such a target to be completed by a worker or bhakta (devotee) that he would have to be a veritable superman to accomplish it. There are many important events in my life related to this. I feel that it happens in every disciple's life whether he is a wholetime worker, LFT or general disciple. Baba used to evaluate their entire lives during RDS. Here I want to clarify that RDS was a collective event but in reality each devotee felt that there was review of only one person because only Baba and that worker knew all the mistakes of the past. By correcting mistakes, giving guidance in health, philosophy and meditation, and by sometimes revealing the events of past lives, He revealed Himself as the all-knowing Divine Father. Therefore, in RDS He used to review all of us, one-by-one, and blessed are those who got the chance to be present there.

It was late in the afternoon when Baba called a meeting for all workers. We were eight in number. Baba at that time in history used to either give Personal Contact or be with workers and margis in His room. This meeting was unusual and at an odd time as well. Baba was sitting on His cot. We all entered the room, prostrated and stood in a half circle around Baba — myself to the right. Silence prevailed for some time. Baba broke the silence.

He said, "What is the duty of a sannyasi (renunciate monk)?" Baba paused and said, "To work for Dharma. You are all sannyasis and you should observe this duty. It is your duty to initiate people into the cult of sadhana; to make them dharmic (righteous moralists) and to unite them. You should not waste a single moment and work for the glory of Dharma."

As I was first to the right, Baba pointed at me and asked, "How many AMPS units have you formed this month?"

The question was unexpected. I gathered my courage and said, "Baba, not this month."

"During the previous month," queried Baba.

"One," I replied in confirmation.

"So this month you utilized for your personal gain only. Humanity is expecting much more from you and you lazy chap did not work for it. If a sannyasi does not work for humanity, then it is not only a condemnable offence but also a punishable one. Stretch out your palm."

Baba's voice was stern and commanding. My mind became blank. Straightaway, I held out both my hands in obedience to His command. By that time, the cane was already in Baba's hand. 'Crack, crack, crack,' went three strokes on the right palm and three more on the left.

I cannot explain what was going on in my mind. I had never been beaten by anyone in my life. Again I remember during my student days that I was beaten by my teacher while quarrelling with another student in class. When my father came to know, he went to school and expressed his anguish.

Thus, all but the one standing at the end received caning on the hands, some six, some twelve. It was a unique experience. The expression on everyone's face was a clear indication of resentment. The reason was clear. None could have imagined this happening, let alone expected it. We had no such experience in the past, not till then in our organizational lives.

Then the turn of the last dada came, Baba was a bit more serious. His face was red. The worker in question had not formed a single unit during the last six months. When asked by Baba to explain, the worker said that since he was posted in a school as principal, he could not spare the time for pracar.

Baba replied, "That was your organizational duty but what about your duty towards Dharma. You neglected it. Hence you should get more punishment. Stretch out your palm." The worker was hesitant, not because he wanted to disobey Baba — the Lord — but because he was afraid of the cane. He had seen us rubbing our palms in pain.

The worker said, "Baba don't hit me, for it will hurt me so much."

Baba said, "Come closer and stretch out your palm." Once only came the sound "crack". The worker retreated back and did not stretch out his palm again for a second rap. Baba thrice asked the worker to do so but he did not do so.

Then what did Baba do? He got down from the cot and started hitting the worker over the body wherever it was accessible to Him.

Then the worker dived under Baba's cot and Baba knelt down and started hitting him under the bed.

The worker caught hold of Baba's stick and refused to release it until Baba assured him that He would not hit him anymore. That was indeed an interesting drama. Baba was kneeling on the ground with His left hand on the cot and His right hand holding the stick, looking towards the worker under the cot who was holding the other end of the stick. Baba would say, "You... let go of the stick."

The worker would reply, "No, I won't leave the stick." Baba asked three times and all three times this worker refused. There was a little change in Baba's tone. It softened and he asked, "Why don't you want to leave the stick?"

"Because you hit me," the worker replied like a small boy. He further said, "I will release the stick only on one condition and that is if you give me promise that you won't hit anymore."

Baba gave the assurance and the stick was released. Baba's face was amused and became normal.

We simply could not control ourselves and burst into laughter. By that time Baba was back sitting upon His cot. He was completely normal. He was smiling.
